# DEFECT REGISTER — every open data/logic concern, with an owner

**Cory, 2026-08-17:** *"it's also your job to make sure nothing gets left behind
or not chased down, especially potential data or logic errors or anything that
messes with our models draft or inseason tools."*

Every row has an **owner** and a **next action**. A row with neither is a defect
in this file. `draft/tests/test_defect_register.py` fails if one appears.

**This file tracks what is BROKEN. `CORY-ASKS.md` tracks what CORY ASKED FOR and
whether he has it** — a defect row and a promise row are different things, and an
ask with no defect behind it used to fall between them.

**Status words mean one thing each:** `OPEN` = nobody has looked since it was
filed · `IN HAND` = someone is on it now · `WAITING` = blocked on a named person
or date · `CLOSED` = fixed and verified, kept for the record.

> change any of them, and changing a date WITH A REASON is a legitimate way to

>
> **SO, PLAINLY: on 08-20, 22 rows go overdue and `main` goes red unless they
> are resolved or re-dated — two days before the draft.** That is the forcing
> function working, not a bug, and it is stated here in advance so nobody
> discovers it at 2am on draft eve. **If that pressure is wrong, the answer is
> to move the dates deliberately and say why — not to mute the check.**

Brown/Lamar/…, and the top-25 median rank delta against the board's own ordering falls **55 slots → 21**. **That comparison was rigged: it measured the two candidate currencies against EACH OTHER, which cannot say which is closer to the ordering the tool already trusts.** The neutral test is each against `vorp`, and it REVERSES on every decision-relevant subset (window ADP≤160, K/DEF excluded; % = cross-position pairs disagreeing with VORP): **all pairs 33.5% → 32.0%** · **a QB on one side 51.6% → 42.0% (better)** · **within 20 ADP of each other 40.1% → 41.8% (WORSE)** · a QB on one side AND within 20 ADP 31.9% → 33.1% (WORSE) · **NO QB either side, the control, 22.8% → 26.1% (WORSE)**. **Re-pricing wins only on the aggregate QB set, which is dominated by pairs nobody weighs against each other — an elite QB against a fringe receiver. Restricted to players Cory could actually choose between it LOSES, and it damages the RB-vs-WR comparison he will mostly use in order to repair the QB one.** **Mechanism, from the formula itself: `boom = ceiling − mean` is replacement-INVARIANT — `(ceil−R)−(mean−R)` is `ceil−mean` — so a level subtraction fixes only the mean half, and near replacement `max(0, mean−R)` collapses toward zero and hands the ranking to a boom term still denominated in raw points, where a QB's absolute spread is ~2× a receiver's. A real fix must re-denominate boom too: a value-model change, not a four-line patch, and not something to attempt four days out.** **SO THE RECOMMENDATION HARDENS TO: REFUSE, NEVER RE-PRICE** — and §6 of the test pins that, with the instruction to keep it even after the refusal ships, because the re-pricing idea will occur to the next reader exactly as it occurred to me. Cory, 2026-08-18: *"this isn't a ceiling…"* — he is right, and it is the cleanest statement of this defect anyone has made.** A ceiling is a property of **one player's** outcome distribution. | 41 | 🟡 **FROM WEEK 1, "no change" ON A WEEKLY CAPTURE IS A SILENT HOLE IN THE SERIES, AND FOUR WORKFLOWS TREAT IT AS BENIGN ALL YEAR.** `weekly-snap-counts.yml`, `weekly-proj-snapshot.yml`, `weekly-routes.yml` and `external-adp-capture.yml` all end with `git diff --cached --quiet && echo "no change" && exit 0`. **The reasoning is right today and stops being right on 09-10.** `weekly-snap-counts.yml` states the stakes itself: *"the value of this feed is `share_volatility` — how much a player's snap share MOVES week to week. That is a quantity you can only build out of a week-by-week series, and a series with holes in it measures a different thing than one without."* **Pre-season, nothing staged means the season is unpublished — correct, and going red weekly until September would train us to ignore the job. In-season, nothing staged means one of three things and the workflow cannot tell them apart: the week was already captured (fine), the source has not published yet (retry), or the fetch/join silently returned nothing (a HOLE, and it is the one that matters).** *"I could not look"* rendered as *"fine"* — `commitments_check.js` calls that BLIND IS NOT QUIET and refuses it; these do not. **Found from the pattern, not from a breakage** (ledger P69's question applied to the scheduled jobs): 24 workflows carry a `schedule:`, **10 have no test that references them at all**, and these four are the subset whose silence would cost a series rather than a run. Nothing is broken today — week 1 is 09-10. | **A** · **D** | OPEN | **A owns the workflows; D owns the in-season series that would carry the hole.** **Make "no change" season-aware before 09-10.** Minimum viable: once the current NFL week is ≥ 1, an empty stage is a WARNING with the week number named, and an empty stage on the SAME week two runs running is a failure. The retry/hole distinction needs the week number, which every one of these fetchers already knows. **Recheck 2026-09-03** — a week before the opener, so a fix has somewhere to land. **⚠️ CORRECTION TO THIS ROW, SAME DAY, AND IT CHANGES THE RECOMMENDATION RATHER THAN THE DEADLINE.** I measured "is this workflow tested?" as *does any file under `draft/tests/` mention its name* — a ONE-DIRECTIONAL PROXY, and it was wrong in both directions. **`standing-check.yml` was on my no-test list and should not have been: it runs `pytest draft/tests/test_sleeper_trending.py` and `node draft/tests/component_write.test.js` INSIDE ITSELF, under the step names "Test the capture and the check before trusting either" and "Test the writer before trusting what it writes".** The relationship runs workflow→test and my grep only looked test→workflow. Re-measured both ways: **24 scheduled workflows · 13 have a test that mentions them · 6 INVOKE a test suite before trusting their own output · 10 have neither** (`data-inventory`, `external-ingest-run`, `inbox-health`, `kalshi-capture`, `mutation-manifest`, `odds-capture`, `sleeper-active-probe`, `weekly-proj-snapshot`, `weekly-routes`, `weekly-snap-counts`). **And "a test mentions the name" is not evidence either** — it is the same proxy pointing the other way. Only the 6 that RUN a suite have anything watching them. **SO THE FIX IS ALREADY HOUSE STYLE AND I DO NOT NEED TO INVENT IT:** `draft-data` (8 suites), `lab` (12), `rehearsals` (5), `self-audit` (4), `standing-check` (2) and `market-capture` (1) all test before they trust. **The recommendation for the four capture workflows is that pattern plus the week-aware empty check — not a new mechanism.** **AND THE SHARPER CUT, which is what is now RATCHETED in `draft/tests/test_every_gate_can_fail.py`:** the question that matters is not *is the workflow mentioned in a test* but *does anything verify what it commits before it commits it*. **24 scheduled workflows · 18 COMMIT data · 5 run a test suite first · 2 delegate to a self-checking script (`own-weekly-proj`, `weekly-grade`) · 11 commit with NOTHING verifying.** Ratcheted at 11 rather than failed, because eleven new reds four days before the draft get switched off by Saturday. **Stated limit, in the test itself:** script detection is a regex over `run:` blocks and misses inline heredocs, so `data-inventory` and `external-ingest-run` read as unverified because they invoke no NAMED script. That may be unfair to those two — a limit of the measurement, not a finding about them — and a test asserts the reason still applies, so if either grows a named script the count gets re-checked rather than silently inherited. **🔴 ESCALATED 2026-08-18 — THIS ROW NOW PROTECTS THE ONLY EXPERIMENT THAT CAN SETTLE THE SOURCE RULING, AND THE FAILURE HAS ALREADY HAPPENED ONCE.** Tracing P39 today established that **we have never measured our model against Sleeper on any season** — the promotion bar is *"beat BOTH NAIVE baselines"*, `api.sleeper.app: "no route"`, and `model_accuracy_v5` says the comparison *"first becomes measurable at the January 2027 grade of the frozen 2026 proj_series"*. **`weekly-proj-snapshot.yml` produces that series. It is not an input to the experiment; it IS the experiment**, and it is the only thing that can ever answer `CORY-ASKS` A2. **AND IT HAS ALREADY LOST A DAY, SILENTLY.** `draft/data/proj_series.json` holds 10 captured dates and **2026-08-10 has a `sleeper` row and NO `fantasypros` row** — FP capture rate 9/10. **Nothing reported it.** That happened in the easiest possible conditions: preseason, daily cadence, no game-week timing pressure. **Retroactive fetches leak (exp33), so it can never be backfilled.** **GUARDED AS OF TODAY:** `draft/tests/test_proj_series_completeness.py` (5 checks) ratchets incomplete dates at the known 1, **names the known hole so a swap cannot hide behind the same count**, carries a fail arm, and states its own limit — it compares sources WITHIN a captured date, so **a day the job never ran at all leaves no row and is still invisible.** That gap needs an expected-cadence field and belongs with the workflow fix, which is this row. **Priority is no longer routine in-season hygiene.** **✅ THE LIMIT I SHIPPED THIS CHECK WITH IS NOW CLOSED, AND IT DID NOT NEED THE FIELD I SAID IT NEEDED.** I wrote that a day the job NEVER RAN leaves no row and is invisible, and that catching it *"needs an expected-cadence field the series does not carry"*. **Wrong — the cadence is knowable from the workflows.** `draft-data.yml` runs `0 8 * * *`, DAILY, and is what appends these rows (`weekly-proj-snapshot.yml` adds the Sunday one). errors were made and corrected the same day: the keeper `cost_round` label read
as a price (retracted in `keeper_surplus_read_2026-08-17.md`), an asymmetric
stress test in the Henry-vs-Olave comparison, and a distinct-ratio count used
where a cv was needed — the last being failure mode (1) in
`constant_multiple_sweep`'s own header. **A lane that audits others must show its
own misses**, and all three are in the audit files rather than quietly fixed.
**And the register itself was the ownership gap.** `DEFECT-REGISTER.md` is **not
on `main`** — it exists only on `claude/fantasy-football-research-926y6z`. The
file whose entire purpose is that nothing gets left behind, and whose test fails
on any row without an owner, is itself behind an unmerged branch. **That is the
mechanical cause of "no one owns anything", and only A can fix it by merging.**
